  "abstract_verbatim": "We construct an infinite group with an ordinary finite presentation in which every element has finite order, answering the finitely presented Burnside question negatively. We also construct an infinite-dimensional finitely presented nonunital nil associative algebra over 𝔽<sub>2</sub> that is Jacobson radical but not nilpotent. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct an infinite group with an ordinary finite presentation in which every element has finite order, answering the finitely presented Burnside question negatively. We also construct an infinite-dimensional finitely presented nonunital nil associative algebra over 𝔽<sub>2</sub> that is Jacobson radical but not nilpotent. Its unitization is finitely presented, algebraic, and infinite-dimensional. These algebras answer the finitely presented nil- and radical-algebra nilpotence questions and the finite-presentation version of Kurosh's algebraic finiteness question negatively.

- **claim-periodic-finite-presentation** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is an infinite periodic group with an ordinary finite presentation. More precisely, the construction below gives a unital associative $\mathbb F_2$-algebra $R$ for which $\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_{12}(R)$ is infinite, finitely presented, and periodic.
  - Explanation: Constructs an infinite periodic Steinberg group with an ordinary finite presentation.
- **claim-periodic-kazhdan-group** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-kazhdan)): The group $\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_{12}(R)$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is infinite, finitely presented, and periodic, and has Kazhdan’s property $(T)$. In particular, it is nonamenable.
  - Explanation: The constructed group also has Kazhdan’s property and is nonamenable.
- **claim-replacement-class-basis** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-basis)): For a presentation of this kind over a field $K$, the nonzero replacement classes of words form a $K$-basis of the algebra. A class is zero precisely when one of its words contains an elementary zero-declared substring. The empty word is one of the surviving classes, and the algebra is graded by word length.
  - Explanation: Replacement components surviving the zero declarations give a basis, and length preservation gives a grading.
- **claim-finite-rule-hierarchy** (construction, [source](#stmt-fr-prop-runs)): There are absolute positive integers $c,k$, with $c=10000$ and $k=10$ permitted, with the following property. Fix an integer $D\geq2$. There is an integer $h_0$ and, for every $$h\in\{h_0,h_0D,h_0D^2,\ldots\},$$ a finite alphabet $\Sigma_h$, of cardinality at most $2^{ch}$, and a finite table of elementary replacements and zero declarations of length at most $k$. Write $A_h(K)$ for the resulting unital associative algebra over a field $K$, and write $[w]$ for the image of a word. These tables have the following three properties.* 1. * There is a fixed-length substitution $E_h:\Sigma_{Dh}\longrightarrow\Sigma_h^{L_h}$, where $L_h=2q_h+1$. Every elementary relation or zero declaration at parameter $Dh$ is a consequence of the relations at parameter $h$ after substitution. Thus $E_h$ induces a unital algebra homomorphism $A_{Dh}(K)\longrightarrow A_h(K)$. If a word $w$ is nonzero at parameter $Dh$, then $E_h(w)$ is nonzero at parameter $h$. There is a nonzero one-letter word at every parameter.* 2. * A subset of $\Sigma_h$ specifies cuts immediately before its letters. There are a bound $B_h$ and a finite list $\mathcal T_h$ of tokens such that every sufficiently long nonzero word has a unique decomposition $$p\,t_1\cdots t_r\,s, \qquad t_i\in\mathcal T_h,\qquad \|p\|,\|s\|\leq B_h.$$ Here $p$ is the part before its first cut, $s$ is the part from its last cut, and the $t_i$ are exactly the intervals between consecutive cuts. Cuts are counted only when their following letter occurs in the word. Each token starts with a cut letter and has no other cut letter. For each $t\in\mathcal T_h$ there is a nonempty word $u_t$ over $\Sigma_{Dh}$ such that $$1\leq \|u_t\|\leq k, \qquad \|t\|=L_h\|u_t\|, \qquad [t]=[E_h(u_t)].$$ The equalities can be effected by replacements supported inside the token. No assertion is made that arbitrary concatenations of tokens are nonzero.* 3. *Some letters are designated palette letters and have a color in a set of $q_h$ colors. An eligible run consists of consecutive palette letters with a common tuple of non-color fields and with neither of the two control-track head flags set. Every adjacent transposition within such a run is an elementary replacement of the whole letters. Every eligible run in a nonzero word has pairwise distinct colors. Every sufficiently long nonzero word contains an eligible run of length at least $q_h/100$. Eligibility and the endpoints of maximal eligible runs depend only on the non-color fields.* *The integer $D$ is chosen before the program fixed point used to define the tables; $h_0$ is chosen afterward. The constants $c,k$ do not depend on $D$.
  - Explanation: Constructs finite rule tables with faithful encodings, isolated token normalizations, and freely permutable distinct-color runs.
- **claim-palette-soundness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-palette-soundness)): A complete palette containing each color exactly once, initially idle on its checker track, cannot reach a palette zero under the palette operations, interleaved arbitrarily with the transaction operations defined below. Conversely, suppose a palette together with its preceding $C(q_h)$ and following $S$ occurs in a nonzero word. Its checker can be removed by replacements supported on those cells. Once its transaction track is also idle, the palette has each color exactly once and can be sorted into increasing color order.
  - Explanation: Complete clean palettes remain safe under interleaving; a complete palette in a nonzero word can be cleared and sorted.
- **claim-context-safe-transactions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-context)): Starting from a concatenation of complete clean blocks, the structural, transaction, and checker syntax is preserved under all elementary replacements. Distinct transactions never own cells of the same block. The elementary moves are context safe at both untouched neighboring letters, even when two transaction regions abut.
  - Explanation: Elementary moves preserve the syntax and keep transaction ownership disjoint even at abutting regions.
- **claim-fixed-payload-verification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-verification)): Fix the data slots and certificate of a complete syntactically admissible transaction region. Using only full-head control moves and starting at $A_1$ with zero registers, a full head can reach success precisely when every clause is true on those fixed bits. No such path reaches a format, nonblank-register, or false-after-gate zero. After a replacement success changes the committed side, verification can be reversed to the acquisition interface.
  - Explanation: A full head started at acquisition reaches success exactly for a satisfying fixed certificate and can reverse after changing sides.
- **claim-arbitrary-state-rollback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-rollback)): A complete transaction region with one full head in any allowed control state can either be returned to acquisition by replacements inside the region, or can reach a declared zero there. This remains true when its control state and registers were not reached from acquisition. A boundary-cursor region can always be released inside its acquired interval.
  - Explanation: Rollback handles forged full-head states as well as states reached through acquisition, and boundary cursors can release their acquired cells.
- **claim-faithful-encoding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fr-faithful)): The substitution $E_h$ simulates every elementary next-parameter replacement and zero declaration. Moreover, if $w$ is a nonzero word at parameter $Dh$, no sequence of lower-parameter replacements starting from $E_h(w)$ can reach a zero declaration.
  - Explanation: Encodings simulate upper rules while preserving nonzero status through arbitrary lower replacements.
- **claim-bounded-fragments** (bound, [source](#stmt-fr-fragment-bound)): There is a bound depending only on $h$ for every palette fragment in a nonzero word. One may take $16q_h+2$. An interval without a cut has bounded length depending only on $h$, including when the interval reaches an end of the word.
  - Explanation: Duplicate-color zeros bound palette fragments, and the transaction syntax bounds intervals without cuts, including word ends.
- **claim-isolated-token-normalization** (construction, [source](#stmt-fr-token-normalization)): An interval between consecutive cuts in a nonzero word consists of between one and $k$ complete structural blocks and can be changed, using only replacements inside that interval, to a concatenation of canonical blocks.
  - Explanation: A cut interval in a nonzero word normalizes internally to a bounded concatenation of canonical blocks.
- **claim-alternating-matrix-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ca-alternating)): For a field $K$ of characteristic two and matrices $X_1,\ldots,X_m\in M_d(K)$, put $$\mathcal A_m(X_1,\ldots,X_m) =\sum_{\sigma\in S_m}X_{\sigma(1)}\cdots X_{\sigma(m)}.$$ If $m>d^2$, then $\mathcal A_m(X_1,\ldots,X_m)=0$.
  - Explanation: Multilinearity and repeated-argument cancellation force the permutation sum to vanish beyond the matrix-space dimension.
- **claim-direct-chunk-cancellation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ca-direct)): Fix $j,d,b$. If $$\frac{q_j}{100}>b(d^2+3), \label{ca:direct-bound}$$ then every dimension-$d$ chunk test of label bound $b$ at level $j$ eventually vanishes over every field of characteristic two.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large palette gives a free permutation packet whose matrix weights cancel in every long chunk sum.
- **claim-cut-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ca-cut-reduction)): Fix a level $j$, a field $K$ of characteristic two, and a dimension $d$. If every dimension-$d$ chunk test of label bound $k$ at level $j+1$ eventually vanishes, then every dimension-$d$ letter test at level $j$ eventually vanishes.
  - Explanation: The unique cut decomposition transfers eventual vanishing from upper chunk tests to lower letter tests.
- **claim-prefix-state-chunk-automaton** (construction, [source](#stmt-ca-automaton)): Let $\Sigma$ be a nonempty finite alphabet of size $s$, and let a chunk test over $\Sigma$ have dimension $d$ and label bound $b$. There is a letter test of dimension at most $$C_b s^b d,\qquad C_b=b, \label{ca:automaton-bound}$$ whose length-$n$ sum has the chunk sum as one fixed $d$ by $d$ block for every $n$. Consequently eventual vanishing of all letter tests in that dimension implies eventual vanishing of the chunk test.
  - Explanation: A finite prefix-state construction realizes chunk sums as a fixed block of letter-test sums and bounds its dimension.
- **claim-fixed-tests-eventually-vanish** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ca-all-tests)): Over every field $K$ of characteristic two, every letter test at level zero eventually vanishes. The same is true of every level-zero chunk test with any fixed finite label bound.
  - Explanation: Palette growth eventually dominates the accumulated automaton dimensions for each fixed test.
- **claim-infinite-matrix-nil-algebra** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-algebra)): There is an infinite, finitely presented, graded unital associative $\mathbb F_2$-algebra $$R=\bigoplus_{n\geq0}R_n=\mathbb F_2\oplus I, \qquad R_0=\mathbb F_2,\qquad I=\bigoplus_{n\geq1}R_n,$$ such that $M_\ell(I)$ is nil for every integer $\ell\geq1$: each matrix with entries in $I$ is nilpotent, with its own nilpotence index.
  - Explanation: The finite homogeneous presentation defines an infinite-dimensional graded algebra whose positive-degree ideal is nil in every finite matrix size.
- **claim-nil-radical-and-algebraic-counterexamples** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-cor-nil-algebra)): For the algebra $R=\mathbb F_2\oplus I$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:algebra">18</a>, the following hold.* 1. *The positive-degree ideal $I$, regarded as a nonunital associative $\mathbb F_2$-algebra, is finitely presented, infinite-dimensional, and nil, but $I^m\ne0$ for every integer $m\geq1$. In particular, it is a finitely presented Jacobson radical algebra that is not nilpotent.* 2. *The algebra $R$ is the unitization of $I$ and is an infinite-dimensional finitely presented algebraic unital associative $\mathbb F_2$-algebra. More precisely, if $r=a1+i$ with $a\in\mathbb F_2$ and $i\in I$, there is an integer $N=N(i)\geq1$ such that $$(r-a1)^N=i^N=0.$$ Thus $r$ satisfies the nonzero monic polynomial $(t-a)^N$.
  - Explanation: The positive-degree ideal is finitely presented, nil and radical without being nilpotent; its unitization is algebraic and infinite-dimensional.
- **claim-explicit-finite-steinberg-presentation** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-finite-presentation)): Let $$R=T/\mathfrak a, \qquad T=\mathbb F_2\langle\mathcal A\rangle, \qquad \mathfrak a=(f_1,\ldots,f_s)$$ be a finite presentation as a unital associative $\mathbb F_2$-algebra, where $\mathfrak a$ denotes the two-sided ideal generated by the listed polynomials. If $n\geq5$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ has the following ordinary finite group presentation.* *The generators are $$g_{ij}(w),\qquad i\neq j,\quad w\in\mathcal A^*,\quad \|w\|\leq2.$$ To specify the relators, define a formal group word $\xi_{ij}(w)$ in these generators for every $w\in\mathcal A^*$. For $\|w\|\leq2$, put $\xi_{ij}(w)=g_{ij}(w)$. For $\|w\|>2$, write $w=av$, with $a\in\mathcal A$ its first letter, choose the least $h\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\setminus\{i,j\}$, and put $$\xi_{ij}(w)=[\xi_{ih}(a),\xi_{hj}(v)].$$ This recursion terminates and gives a finite group word for each $w$. The defining relations are $$\begin{aligned} g_{ij}(w)^2&=1, && \|w\|\leq2, \label{fp:finite-square}\\ [g_{ij}(u),g_{kl}(v)]&=1, && \|u\|,\|v\|\leq2,\quad j\neq k,\ i\neq l, \label{fp:finite-commutation}\\ [g_{ij}(u),g_{jk}(v)]&=g_{ik}(uv), && \|u\|+\|v\|\leq2,\quad i,j,k\text{ pairwise distinct}, \label{fp:finite-product}\\ \prod_{w\in S_{f_t}}\xi_{ij}(w)&=1, && 1\leq t\leq s,\quad i\neq j. \label{fp:finite-algebra-relators} \end{aligned}$$ The product in Equation <a href="#fp:finite-algebra-relators" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fp:finite-algebra-relators">[fp:finite-algebra-relators]</a> is taken in the fixed order above, and an empty product is $1$. The isomorphism to $\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ sends $g_{ij}(w)$ to $x_{ij}(\overline w)$, where $\overline w$ is the image of $w$ in $R$.
  - Explanation: Builds finitely many group generators and relators from a finite unital algebra presentation, using recursive monomial root words.
- **claim-three-factor-commutator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-three-factor)): Let $X,Y,Z$ belong to a group, and put $A=[X,Y]$ and $B=[Y,Z]$. Assume that $A$ commutes with $X$ and $Y$, that $B$ commutes with $Y$ and $Z$, and that $A$ commutes with $B$ and $X$ commutes with $Z$. Then $$[A,Z]=[X,B].$$ Moreover, this common element centralizes each of $A,Z,X,B$.
  - Explanation: Under the stated commuting assumptions the nested commutators agree and centralize all their input factors.
- **claim-perfectness-and-infinitude** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fp-perfectness)): For every unital associative $\mathbb F_2$-algebra $R$ and $n\geq3$, the groups $\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_n(R)$ are perfect. If $R$ is infinite, both groups are infinite.
  - Explanation: Every root is a commutator, and distinct ring parameters give distinct elementary matrices.
- **claim-matrix-group-periodicity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fp-matrix-periodicity)): Suppose that $R=\mathbb F_2\oplus I$ as a vector space, where $I$ is a two-sided ideal and the indicated copy of $\mathbb F_2$ contains the identity of $R$. Suppose that $M_d(I)$ is a nil algebra for every positive integer $d$; that is, each individual matrix in $M_d(I)$ is nilpotent. Then a square matrix over $R$ is invertible if and only if its reduction modulo $I$ is invertible. Moreover, every element of $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n(R)$ has finite order, and therefore $\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_n(R)$ is periodic.
  - Explanation: Matrix nilpotence lifts invertibility from the residue field and turns finite residue orders into finite orders over the algebra.
- **claim-frame-completion-and-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-linear-algebra)): A square matrix over $R$ is invertible if and only if its reduction modulo $I$ is invertible. Every tuple of columns whose reductions are linearly independent extends to an invertible matrix. The group $H_n$ is generated by elementary matrices and the diagonal matrices $d_i(u)$ having a unit $u\in R^\times$ in one position and ones elsewhere.
  - Explanation: Residue invertibility allows frame completion and elimination using elementary matrices and unit diagonals.
- **claim-frame-connectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-frames)): The complex $X_n$ has vanishing reduced rational homology in degrees at most $n-2$. It is simply connected for $n\geq3$.
  - Explanation: Finite labeled independence complexes give rational homology vanishing and simple connectivity of the frame complex.
- **claim-small-coordinate-matrix-presentation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-small-presentation)): For $n\geq5$, the group $H_n$ has the presentation with elementary generators $e_{ij}(a)$ and diagonal generators $d_i(u)$, subject to all true matrix relations supported on coordinate sets of size at most five.
  - Explanation: Frame transport reduces the full matrix group presentation to true relations on small coordinate supports.
- **claim-central-steinberg-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-centrality)): For $n\geq6$, the subgroup $J_n$ is central in $\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$.
  - Explanation: Faithful strips and a spare coordinate make the matrix action on the Steinberg group well-defined and force the kernel to be central.
- **claim-stable-elementary-quotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-quotient)): The group $\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_n(R)$ is normal in $H_n$. For $n\geq5$, inclusion induces an isomorphism $$H_n/\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_n(R)\longrightarrow H_{n+1}/\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_{n+1}(R).$$
  - Explanation: Elementary matrices form a normal subgroup, and the quotient presentation by unit generators is unchanged under stabilization.
- **claim-locally-finite-rational-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-finite-splitting)): Every central extension of a locally finite group by the additive group $\mathbb Q$ has a unique homomorphic splitting.
  - Explanation: Averaging splits finite quotient extensions, and uniqueness makes the splittings compatible over locally finite quotients.
- **claim-rational-extension-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-st-extensions)): For $n\geq6$, equivalence classes of central extensions of $\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_n(R)$ by the additive group $\mathbb Q$ are naturally in bijection with $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(J_n,\mathbb Q)$. This bijection commutes with restriction along inclusions of ranks.
  - Explanation: Canonical lifts from unitriangular subgroups identify rational central extensions with rational characters of the kernel, naturally in rank.
- **claim-locally-finite-coinvariants** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-coinvariants)): Let $N$ be a locally finite normal subgroup of $K$, and let $M$ be a left $\mathbb Q[K]$-module. There are natural isomorphisms $$H_q(K;M)\cong H_q(K/N;M_N),$$ where $M_N$ denotes the module of $N$-coinvariants.
  - Explanation: Exactness of rational coinvariants identifies homology with that of the quotient and its coinvariant coefficient module.
- **claim-oriented-frame-stabilizers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-stabilizers)): Let $m\geq13$, put $G=\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_m(R)$, and let $1\leq l\leq4$. The group $G$ acts transitively on ordered $l$-frames. Put $r=m-l$, and let $K_l$ be the stabilizer of the unordered frame $\{e_{r+1},\ldots,e_m\}$. Its complementary-block projection gives a split exact sequence $$1\longrightarrow N_l\longrightarrow K_l \longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_r(R)\longrightarrow1,$$ where $N_l$ is locally finite. Let $\mathbb Q_{\epsilon_l}$ be the one-dimensional orientation module of this frame. Then $$H_q(K_l;\mathbb Q_{\epsilon_l})= \begin{cases} H_q(\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_{m-1}(R);\mathbb Q),&l=1,\\ 0,&2\leq l\leq4, \end{cases}$$ where in the first case the identification is induced by the complementary inclusion.
  - Explanation: The stabilizer projects onto a complementary elementary group with locally finite kernel; orientation signs kill the homology of the larger frames.
- **claim-second-rational-homology-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-st-h2)): For $n\geq12$, the standard inclusion induces an isomorphism $$H_2(\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_n(R);\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H_2(\mathop{\mathrm{E}}_{n+1}(R);\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: The frame double complex and vanishing oriented stabilizer columns identify the standard stabilization on second rational homology as an isomorphism.
- **claim-rational-character-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rational-stability)): For $n\geq12$, restriction along $J_n\to J_{n+1}$ induces an isomorphism, and in particular a surjection, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(J_{n+1},\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(J_n,\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: Naturality of the extension classification transfers homology stability to extension of rational kernel characters.
- **claim-rational-detection-of-torsion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-rational-detection)): Let $A$ be an abelian group and $a\in A$. If every homomorphism $A\longrightarrow\mathbb Q$ kills $a$, then $a$ has finite order.
  - Explanation: A nontorsion element survives rationalization and is detected by a rational linear functional.
- **claim-ground-field-kernel-torsion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-field-kernel)): For $n\geq 6$, the group $J_n(\mathbb F_2)$ is torsion.
  - Explanation: Rational central extensions of the finite elementary image split, and perfectness forces every rational character of the kernel to vanish.
- **claim-exact-coordinate-permutation-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-kt-permutations)): Let $B$ be a unital algebra over $\mathbb F_2$, and let $q\geq 3$. Every permutation $\sigma$ of the $q$ coordinates has a lift $p_\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_q(B)$ such that $$p_\sigma x_{ij}(a)p_\sigma^{-1} =x_{\sigma(i)\sigma(j)}(a) \qquad(i\ne j,\ a\in B).$$
  - Explanation: Products of root elements lift coordinate permutations with the exact desired conjugation action on roots.
- **claim-block-root-laws** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-block-laws)): The block roots satisfy $$\begin{aligned} X_{ij}(A+B)&=X_{ij}(A)X_{ij}(B),\\ [X_{ij}(A),X_{kl}(B)]&=1 &&(j\ne k,\ i\ne l),\\ [X_{ij}(A),X_{jk}(B)]&=X_{ik}(AB) &&(i,j,k\text{ distinct}). \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, an elementary change of basis inside one outer block acts on every cross-block root by the exact corresponding row or column operation. In particular, if $P\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_N(\mathbb F_2)$ and $B$ is the matrix with a copy of $P$ in each outer diagonal block, there is a lift $b\in\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_{nN}(R)$ such that $$b^{-1}X_{ij}(A)b=X_{ij}(P^{-1}AP) \qquad(i\ne j).$$
  - Explanation: Cross-block roots satisfy the Steinberg relations, and numerical changes of basis lift to exact conjugation identities.
- **claim-inner-triangular-faithfulness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-triangular-faithfulness)): The natural map from $U$ to matrices is injective. A root whose two inner indices agree normalizes $U$.
  - Explanation: Collection by positive inner gaps gives a faithful triangular subgroup, normalized by equal-inner-index roots.
- **claim-triangular-substitution-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-triangular-evaluation)): If $T$ is upper triangular with all diagonal entries equal to $\lambda\in\mathbb F_2$, then $$W_v(T)=\prod_{s=1}^{N}\iota_s(v(\lambda)).$$
  - Explanation: Strictly upper terms collect into the faithful triangular subgroup and vanish because the matrix image is the identity.
- **claim-cyclic-wrap-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-wrap-removal)): Let $v(t)$ be a product of $m\geq 1$ root factors with polynomial entries of degrees at most $d$, and suppose its elementary matrix product is the identity over $R[t]$. Set $\delta=\max\{1,d\}$. For any integer $$N>2(m+1)\delta,$$ let $C_N$ be the cyclic shift with entries from row $s$ to column $s+1$ modulo $N$, and let $S_N$ be the truncated shift with entries from row $s$ to column $s+1$ for $s<N$. Then $$W_v(C_N)=W_v(S_N)\quad\hbox{in }\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_{nN}(R).$$
  - Explanation: Wrap corrections remain in a faithful strip with disjoint endpoints, so their trivial matrix image forces equality of the substituted Steinberg words.
- **claim-torsion-after-stabilization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-stable-torsion)): Let $n\geq 6$ and $u\in J_n(R)$. The image of $u$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_q(R)$ is torsion for some $q\geq n$ under the standard coordinate homomorphism.
  - Explanation: Grading and cyclic versus truncated substitutions make a stabilized kernel element torsion without assuming stabilization injective.
- **claim-fixed-rank-kernel-torsion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kt-kernel-torsion)): For every $n\geq 12$, the group $J_n(R)$ is torsion.
  - Explanation: Every rational character extends to a rank where the element is torsion; rational detection then proves torsion in the original central kernel.

Thus $E_h$ induces a unital algebra homomorphism $A_{Dh}(K)\\longrightarrow A_h(K)$. If a word $w$ is nonzero at parameter $Dh$, then $E_h(w)$ is nonzero at parameter $h$. There is a nonzero one-letter word at every parameter.*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"fr:prop-tokens\" label=\"fr:prop-tokens\"></span> A subset of $\\Sigma_h$ specifies cuts immediately before its letters. Cuts are counted only when their following letter occurs in the word. Each token starts with a cut letter and has no other cut letter. For each $t\\in\\mathcal T_h$ there is a nonempty word $u_t$ over $\\Sigma_{Dh}$ such that $$1\\leq |u_t|\\leq k,\n     \\qquad |t|=L_h|u_t|,\n     \\qquad [t]=[E_h(u_t)].$$ The equalities can be effected by replacements supported inside the token. No assertion is made that arbitrary concatenations of tokens are nonzero.*\n\n3.  *Some letters are designated palette letters and have a color in a set of $q_h$ colors. An eligible run consists of consecutive palette letters with a common tuple of non-color fields and with neither of the two control-track head flags set. Every adjacent transposition within such a run is an elementary replacement of the whole letters. Every eligible run in a nonzero word has pairwise distinct colors. Every sufficiently long nonzero word contains an eligible run of length at least $q_h/100$. Eligibility and the endpoints of maximal eligible runs depend only on the non-color fields.*\n\n*The integer $D$ is chosen before the program fixed point used to define the tables; $h_0$ is chosen afterward. The constants $c,k$ do not depend on $D$.",

Thus $E_h$ induces a unital algebra homomorphism $A_{Dh}(K)\\longrightarrow A_h(K)$. If a word $w$ is nonzero at parameter $Dh$, then $E_h(w)$ is nonzero at parameter $h$. There is a nonzero one-letter word at every parameter.*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"fr:prop-tokens\" label=\"fr:prop-tokens\"></span> A subset of $\\Sigma_h$ specifies cuts immediately before its letters. There are a bound $B_h$ and a finite list $\\mathcal T_h$ of tokens such that every sufficiently long nonzero word has a unique decomposition $$p\\,t_1\\cdots t_r\\,s,\n     \\qquad t_i\\in\\mathcal T_h,\\qquad |p|,|s|\\leq B_h.$$ Here $p$ is the part before its first cut, $s$ is the part from its last cut, and the $t_i$ are exactly the intervals between consecutive cuts. Cuts are counted only when their following letter occurs in the word. Each token starts with a cut letter and has no other cut letter. For each $t\\in\\mathcal T_h$ there is a nonempty word $u_t$ over $\\Sigma_{Dh}$ such that $$1\\leq |u_t|\\leq k,\n     \\qquad |t|=L_h|u_t|,\n     \\qquad [t]=[E_h(u_t)].$$ The equalities can be effected by replacements supported inside the token. No assertion is made that arbitrary concatenations of tokens are nonzero.*\n\n3.  *Some letters are designated palette letters and have a color in a set of $q_h$ colors. An eligible run consists of consecutive palette letters with a common tuple of non-color fields and with neither of the two control-track head flags set. Every adjacent transposition within such a run is an elementary replacement of the whole letters. Every eligible run in a nonzero word has pairwise distinct colors. Every sufficiently long nonzero word contains an eligible run of length at least $q_h/100$. Eligibility and the endpoints of maximal eligible runs depend only on the non-color fields.*\n\n*The integer $D$ is chosen before the program fixed point used to define the tables; $h_0$ is chosen afterward. The constants $c,k$ do not depend on $D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-247.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"prop:hierarchy\" label=\"prop:hierarchy\"></span> There are absolute positive integers $c,k$, with $c=10000$ and $k=10$ permitted, with the following property. Fix an integer $D\\geq2$. There is an integer $h_0$ and, for every $$h\\in\\{h_0,h_0D,h_0D^2,\\ldots\\},$$ a finite alphabet $\\Sigma_h$, of cardinality at most $2^{ch}$, and a finite table of elementary replacements and zero declarations of length at most $k$. Write $A_h(K)$ for the resulting unital associative algebra over a field $K$, and write $[w]$ for the image of a word. These tables have the following three properties.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"fr:prop-encoding\" label=\"fr:prop-encoding\"></span> There is a fixed-length substitution $E_h:\\Sigma_{Dh}\\longrightarrow\\Sigma_h^{L_h}$, where $L_h=2q_h+1$. Every elementary relation or zero declaration at parameter $Dh$ is a consequence of the relations at parameter $h$ after substitution. Thus $E_h$ induces a unital algebra homomorphism $A_{Dh}(K)\\longrightarrow A_h(K)$. If a word $w$ is nonzero at parameter $Dh$, then $E_h(w)$ is nonzero at parameter $h$. There is a nonzero one-letter word at every parameter.*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"fr:prop-tokens\" label=\"fr:prop-tokens\"></span> A subset of $\\Sigma_h$ specifies cuts immediately before its letters. There are a bound $B_h$ and a finite list $\\mathcal T_h$ of tokens such that every sufficiently long nonzero word has a unique decomposition $$p\\,t_1\\cdots t_r\\,s,\n     \\qquad t_i\\in\\mathcal T_h,\\qquad |p|,|s|\\leq B_h.$$ Here $p$ is the part before its first cut, $s$ is the part from its last cut, and the $t_i$ are exactly the intervals between consecutive cuts. Cuts are counted only when their following letter occurs in the word. Each token starts with a cut letter and has no other cut letter. For each $t\\in\\mathcal T_h$ there is a nonempty word $u_t$ over $\\Sigma_{Dh}$ such that $$1\\leq |u_t|\\leq k,\n     \\qquad |t|=L_h|u_t|,\n     \\qquad [t]=[E_h(u_t)].$$ The equalities can be effected by replacements supported inside the token. No assertion is made that arbitrary concatenations of tokens are nonzero.*\n\n3.  *Some letters are designated palette letters and have a color in a set of $q_h$ colors. An eligible run consists of consecutive palette letters with a common tuple of non-color fields and with neither of the two control-track head flags set. Every adjacent transposition within such a run is an elementary replacement of the whole letters. Every eligible run in a nonzero word has pairwise distinct colors. Every sufficiently long nonzero word contains an eligible run of length at least $q_h/100$. Eligibility and the endpoints of maximal eligible runs depend only on the non-color fields.*\n\n*The integer $D$ is chosen before the program fixed point used to define the tables; $h_0$ is chosen afterward. The constants $c,k$ do not depend on $D$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Sigma$ be a nonempty finite alphabet of size $s$, and let a chunk test over $\\Sigma$ have dimension $d$ and label bound $b$. There is a letter test of dimension at most $$C_b s^b d,\\qquad C_b=b,\n  \\label{ca:automaton-bound}$$ whose length-$n$ sum has the chunk sum as one fixed $d$ by $d$ block for every $n$. Consequently eventual vanishing of all letter tests in that dimension implies eventual vanishing of the chunk test.",
        "source_id": "PM-247.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ca-automaton",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Sigma$ be a nonempty finite alphabet of size $s$, and let a chunk test over $\\Sigma$ have dimension $d$ and label bound $b$. There is a letter test of dimension at most $$C_b s^b d,\\qquad C_b=b,\n  \\label{ca:automaton-bound}$$ whose length-$n$ sum has the chunk sum as one fixed $d$ by $d$ block for every $n$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "There is an infinite, finitely presented, graded unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra $$R=\\bigoplus_{n\\geq0}R_n=\\mathbb F_2\\oplus I,\n  \\qquad R_0=\\mathbb F_2,\\qquad I=\\bigoplus_{n\\geq1}R_n,$$ such that $M_\\ell(I)$ is nil for every integer $\\ell\\geq1$: each matrix with entries in $I$ is nilpotent, with its own nilpotence index.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-nil-algebra",
        "exact_quote": "For the algebra $R=\\mathbb F_2\\oplus I$ of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:algebra\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:algebra\">18</a>, the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The positive-degree ideal $I$, regarded as a nonunital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra, is finitely presented, infinite-dimensional, and nil, but $I^m\\ne0$ for every integer $m\\geq1$. In particular, it is a finitely presented Jacobson radical algebra that is not nilpotent.*\n\n2.  *The algebra $R$ is the unitization of $I$ and is an infinite-dimensional finitely presented algebraic unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra. More precisely, if $r=a1+i$ with $a\\in\\mathbb F_2$ and $i\\in I$, there is an integer $N=N(i)\\geq1$ such that $$(r-a1)^N=i^N=0.$$ Thus $r$ satisfies the nonzero monic polynomial $(t-a)^N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-247.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-nil-algebra",
          "exact_quote": "For the algebra $R=\\mathbb F_2\\oplus I$ of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:algebra\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:algebra\">18</a>, the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The positive-degree ideal $I$, regarded as a nonunital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra, is finitely presented, infinite-dimensional, and nil, but $I^m\\ne0$ for every integer $m\\geq1$. In particular, it is a finitely presented Jacobson radical algebra that is not nilpotent.*\n\n2.  *The algebra $R$ is the unitization of $I$ and is an infinite-dimensional finitely presented algebraic unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra.           "source_id": "PM-247.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-presentation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$R=T/\\mathfrak a,\n \\qquad\n T=\\mathbb F_2\\langle\\mathcal A\\rangle,\n \\qquad\n \\mathfrak a=(f_1,\\ldots,f_s)$$ be a finite presentation as a unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra, where $\\mathfrak a$ denotes the two-sided ideal generated by the listed polynomials. If $n\\geq5$, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ has the following ordinary finite group presentation.*\n\n\n\n\n*The generators are $$g_{ij}(w),\\qquad i\\neq j,\\quad w\\in\\mathcal A^*,\\quad |w|\\leq2.$$ To specify the relators, define a formal group word $\\xi_{ij}(w)$ in these generators for every $w\\in\\mathcal A^*$. For $|w|\\leq2$, put $\\xi_{ij}(w)=g_{ij}(w)$. For $|w|>2$, write $w=av$, with $a\\in\\mathcal A$ its first letter, choose the least $h\\in\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}\\setminus\\{i,j\\}$, and put $$\\xi_{ij}(w)=[\\xi_{ih}(a),\\xi_{hj}(v)].$$ This recursion terminates and gives a finite group word for each $w$. The defining relations are $$\\begin{aligned}\n g_{ij}(w)^2&=1,\n && |w|\\leq2, \\label{fp:finite-square}\\\\\n [g_{ij}(u),g_{kl}(v)]&=1,\n && |u|,|v|\\leq2,\\quad j\\neq k,\\ i\\neq l,\n \\label{fp:finite-commutation}\\\\\n [g_{ij}(u),g_{jk}(v)]&=g_{ik}(uv),\n && |u|+|v|\\leq2,\\quad i,j,k\\text{ pairwise distinct},\n \\label{fp:finite-product}\\\\\n \\prod_{w\\in S_{f_t}}\\xi_{ij}(w)&=1,\n && 1\\leq t\\leq s,\\quad i\\neq j.\n \\label{fp:finite-algebra-relators}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The product in Equation <a href=\"#fp:finite-algebra-relators\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"fp:finite-algebra-relators\">[fp:finite-algebra-relators]</a> is taken in the fixed order above, and an empty product is $1$. The isomorphism to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ sends $g_{ij}(w)$ to $x_{ij}(\\overline w)$, where $\\overline w$ is the image of $w$ in $R$.",
        "source_id": "PM-247.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-presentation",

If $n\\geq5$, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ has the following ordinary finite group presentation.*\n\n\n\n\n*The generators are $$g_{ij}(w),\\qquad i\\neq j,\\quad w\\in\\mathcal A^*,\\quad |w|\\leq2.$$ To specify the relators, define a formal group word $\\xi_{ij}(w)$ in these generators for every $w\\in\\mathcal A^*$. For $|w|\\leq2$, put $\\xi_{ij}(w)=g_{ij}(w)$. For $|w|>2$, write $w=av$, with $a\\in\\mathcal A$ its first letter, choose the least $h\\in\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}\\setminus\\{i,j\\}$, and put $$\\xi_{ij}(w)=[\\xi_{ih}(a),\\xi_{hj}(v)].$$ This recursion terminates and gives a finite group word for each $w$. The defining relations are $$\\begin{aligned}\n g_{ij}(w)^2&=1,\n && |w|\\leq2, \\label{fp:finite-square}\\\\\n [g_{ij}(u),g_{kl}(v)]&=1,\n && |u|,|v|\\leq2,\\quad j\\neq k,\\ i\\neq l,\n \\label{fp:finite-commutation}\\\\\n [g_{ij}(u),g_{jk}(v)]&=g_{ik}(uv),\n && |u|+|v|\\leq2,\\quad i,j,k\\text{ pairwise distinct},\n \\label{fp:finite-product}\\\\\n \\prod_{w\\in S_{f_t}}\\xi_{ij}(w)&=1,\n && 1\\leq t\\leq s,\\quad i\\neq j.\n \\label{fp:finite-algebra-relators}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The product in Equation <a href=\"#fp:finite-algebra-relators\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"fp:finite-algebra-relators\">[fp:finite-algebra-relators]</a> is taken in the fixed order above, and an empty product is $1$. The isomorphism to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ sends $g_{ij}(w)$ to $x_{ij}(\\overline w)$, where $\\overline w$ is the image of $w$ in $R$.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $$R=T/\\mathfrak a,\n \\qquad\n T=\\mathbb F_2\\langle\\mathcal A\\rangle,\n \\qquad\n \\mathfrak a=(f_1,\\ldots,f_s)$$ be a finite presentation as a unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra, where $\\mathfrak a$ denotes the two-sided ideal generated by the listed polynomials.         "exact_quote": "Let $X,Y,Z$ belong to a group, and put $A=[X,Y]$ and $B=[Y,Z]$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fp-matrix-periodicity",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $R=\\mathbb F_2\\oplus I$ as a vector space, where $I$ is a two-sided ideal and the indicated copy of $\\mathbb F_2$ contains the identity of $R$. Suppose that $M_d(I)$ is a nil algebra for every positive integer $d$; that is, each individual matrix in $M_d(I)$ is nilpotent. Then a square matrix over $R$ is invertible if and only if its reduction modulo $I$ is invertible. Moreover, every element of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_n(R)$ has finite order, and therefore $\\mathop{\\mathrm{E}}_n(R)$ is periodic.",
        "source_id": "PM-247.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fp-matrix-periodicity",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $R=\\mathbb F_2\\oplus I$ as a vector space, where $I$ is a two-sided ideal and the indicated copy of $\\mathbb F_2$ contains the identity of $R$.           "source_id": "PM-247.2"

        "exact_quote": "A square matrix over $R$ is invertible if and only if its reduction modulo $I$ is invertible.       "exact_statement": "A square matrix over $R$ is invertible if and only if its reduction modulo $I$ is invertible.         "exact_quote": "Let $m\\geq13$, put $G=\\mathop{\\mathrm{E}}_m(R)$, and let $1\\leq l\\leq4$. The group $G$ acts transitively on ordered $l$-frames. Put $r=m-l$, and let $K_l$ be the stabilizer of the unordered frame $\\{e_{r+1},\\ldots,e_m\\}$. Its complementary-block projection gives a split exact sequence $$1\\longrightarrow N_l\\longrightarrow K_l\n \\longrightarrow\\mathop{\\mathrm{E}}_r(R)\\longrightarrow1,$$ where $N_l$ is locally finite. Let $\\mathbb Q_{\\epsilon_l}$ be the one-dimensional orientation module of this frame. Then $$H_q(K_l;\\mathbb Q_{\\epsilon_l})=\n \\begin{cases}\n H_q(\\mathop{\\mathrm{E}}_{m-1}(R);\\mathbb Q),&l=1,\\\\\n 0,&2\\leq l\\leq4,\n \\end{cases}$$ where in the first case the identification is induced by the complementary inclusion.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-kt-block-laws",
        "exact_quote": "The block roots satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n X_{ij}(A+B)&=X_{ij}(A)X_{ij}(B),\\\\\n [X_{ij}(A),X_{kl}(B)]&=1\n           &&(j\\ne k,\\ i\\ne l),\\\\\n [X_{ij}(A),X_{jk}(B)]&=X_{ik}(AB)\n           &&(i,j,k\\text{ distinct}).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, an elementary change of basis inside one outer block acts on every cross-block root by the exact corresponding row or column operation. In particular, if $P\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_N(\\mathbb F_2)$ and $B$ is the matrix with a copy of $P$ in each outer diagonal block, there is a lift $b\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_{nN}(R)$ such that $$b^{-1}X_{ij}(A)b=X_{ij}(P^{-1}AP)\n \\qquad(i\\ne j).$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Periodic group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A group is *periodic* if every element has finite order.
- **Ordinary finite presentation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An *ordinary finite presentation* specifies finitely many generators and finitely many relator words, and takes the quotient of the free group by their normal closure.
- **Nil and nilpotent nonunital algebras** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For an associative algebra $A$ without an identity, *nil* means that each $a\in A$ has some power equal to zero, while *nilpotent* means that $A^N=0$ for one integer $N\geq1$.
- **Nonunital finite algebra presentation** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A finite presentation in the nonunital category is a quotient of a free nonunital associative algebra on finitely many generators by a two-sided ideal generated by finitely many relations.
- **Jacobson radical algebra** ([source](#pm-h-5)): An algebra is Jacobson radical when every element is quasi-regular, equivalently when $1-a$ is invertible in its unitization for every element $a$.
- **Algebraic algebra** ([source](#pm-h-5)): An algebra over a field is *algebraic* if each element satisfies a nonzero polynomial over that field.
- **Elementary replacement** ([source](#sec:finite-rules)): An elementary replacement is a relation $v=v'$ between nonempty words of the same length; it may be used in either direction inside a word.
- **Elementary zero declaration** ([source](#sec:finite-rules)): An elementary zero declaration is a relation $v=0$, with $v$ nonempty.
- **Clean and canonical block** ([source](#fr:formats)): A *clean block* has both tracks idle and has each palette color exactly once. It is *canonical* when those colors occur in increasing order.
- **Weighted letter and chunk tests** ([source](#stmt-ca-tests)): A *letter test* of dimension $d\geq1$ at level $j$ assigns a matrix $M(a)\in M_d(K)$ to each $a\in\Sigma_j$. For $w=a_1\cdots a_n$ put $M(w)=M(a_1)\cdots M(a_n)$ and $M(\varnothing)=1_d$. Its length-$n$ sum is $$S_{j,n}(M)=\sum_{w\in\Sigma_j^n} M(w)\otimes[w]_j. \label{ca:letter-sum}$$ A *chunk test* of dimension $d$ and label bound $b\geq1$ consists of a finite set $\mathcal C$ of chunk identifiers, a nonempty label $v_\gamma\in\Sigma_j^*$ with $\|v_\gamma\|\leq b$ for each $\gamma\in\mathcal C$, and a weight $W_\gamma\in M_d(K)$. Its length-$n$ sum is $$C_{j,n}(\mathcal C,W)= \sum_{\substack{r\geq0,\ \gamma_1,\ldots,\gamma_r\in\mathcal C\\ \|v_{\gamma_1}\|+\cdots+\|v_{\gamma_r}\|=n}} W_{\gamma_1}\cdots W_{\gamma_r} \otimes[v_{\gamma_1}\cdots v_{\gamma_r}]_j. \label{ca:chunk-sum}$$ The empty sequence contributes $1_d\otimes1$ when $n=0$. A test *eventually vanishes* if its length-$n$ sum is zero for every sufficiently large integer $n$.
- **Steinberg kernel** ([source](#fp:kernel-definition)): $$J_n(R)=\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\pi_n. \label{fp:kernel-definition}$$ When the algebra is understood, we omit $R$ from these group names.
- **Block roots** ([source](#pm-h-39)): For $i\ne j$ and a matrix $A=(a_{st})\in M_N(R)$, put $$X_{ij}(A)=\prod_{s,t=1}^{N}x_{(i,s),(j,t)}(a_{st}) \quad\hbox{in }\mathop{\mathrm{St}}_{nN}(R).$$

      "exact_definition": "For $i\\ne j$ and a matrix $A=(a_{st})\\in M_N(R)$, put $$X_{ij}(A)=\\prod_{s,t=1}^{N}x_{(i,s),(j,t)}(a_{st})\n       \\quad\\hbox{in }\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_{nN}(R).$$",
      "interpretation": "A rectangular block parameter is represented by commuting scalar roots between different outer coordinate blocks.",
      "kind": "notation",
      "name": "Block roots",
      "scope": "Finite matrix substitutions."
    }
  ],

}

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-alphabet-layout-constant | Alphabet bit-length multiplier | = | $10000$ | A permitted absolute choice independent of the hierarchy multiplier. |
| q-rule-length-constant | Permitted elementary relation length bound | = | $10$ | Absolute bound independent of the hierarchy multiplier. |
| q-alphabet-cardinality | Alphabet cardinality | ≤ | $2^{ch}$ | Parameters in the stated multiplicative sequence after choosing the program. |
| q-encoding-block-length | Length of an encoded upper letter | = | $2q_h+1$ | One core, one complete palette, and a stop at a fixed hierarchy parameter. |
| q-palette-fragment-bound | Palette fragment length in a nonzero word | ≤ | $16q_h+2$ | Fixed hierarchy parameter. |
| q-cut-free-interval-bound | Cut-free interval length | ≤ | $(k+2)(17q_h+3)$ | Fixed hierarchy parameter and relation-length bound. |
| q-hierarchy-growth-condition | Hierarchy multiplier | > | $10(c+1)(k+1)$ | Chosen before the program fixed point and the initial hierarchy parameter. |
| q-alternating-sum-threshold | Number of matrix arguments sufficient for alternating cancellation | > | $d^2$ | Fixed matrix dimension. |
| q-direct-cancellation-threshold | Palette run scale required for direct cancellation | > | $b(d^2+3)$ | Fixed level, test dimension, and finite label bound. |
| q-prefix-automaton-dimension | Letter-test dimension realizing a chunk test | ≤ | $C_b s^b d$ | Alphabet size, chunk dimension, and maximum label length. |
| q-prefix-state-count | Number of prefix states | ≤ | $C_b s^b$ | A finite nonempty alphabet and positive label bound. |
| q-finite-presentation-rank | Rank sufficient for the explicit finite Steinberg presentation | ≥ | $5$ | Sufficient rank for the displayed construction; no minimality is asserted. |
| q-centrality-rank | Rank sufficient for centrality of the Steinberg kernel | ≥ | $6$ | Sufficient rank for the centrality argument. |
| q-frame-homology-range | Degree through which reduced rational frame homology vanishes | ≤ | $n-2$ | Ambient column dimension. |
| q-rational-stability-rank | Rank sufficient for rational kernel-character stability | ≥ | $12$ | Sufficient rank for the rational restriction isomorphism. |
| q-wrap-removal-size | Inner matrix size sufficient for wrap removal | > | $2(m+1)\delta$ | Root-factor count and maximum polynomial degree. |
| q-kernel-torsion-rank | Rank sufficient for torsion of the constructed Steinberg kernel | ≥ | $12$ | Sufficient fixed rank; no stabilization injectivity is assumed. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-word-basis** [claim](#stmt-fr-basis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-basis): Quotient the word graph by replacement components and then remove components meeting zero declarations. -> A homogeneous basis of surviving replacement classes.
- **step-palette-safety** [claim](#stmt-fr-palette-soundness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-palette-soundness): Track the color multiset and reverse the checker through its passed prefix. -> Palette safety and local clearing and sorting.
- **step-context-safety** [claim](#stmt-fr-context) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-context): Check untouched interfaces for creation, acquisition, release, movement, and checker operations. -> Preserved syntax and disjoint block ownership.
- **step-verifier-correctness** [claim](#stmt-fr-verification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-verification): Read fixed addressed bits, test the gate, and undo the register changes along an invertible control path. -> Success exactly for a true certificate, with reversible verification.
- **step-forged-state-rollback** [claim](#stmt-fr-rollback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-rollback): Reverse each finite tour and decrease the clause index, exposing semantic zeros when interface conditions fail. -> Release to acquisition or an internal zero witness.
- **step-rule-table-fixed-point** [claim](#stmt-fr-prop-runs) / [proof](#fr:fixed-point): Encode accepting computations by addressable clauses and construct a fixed program that decides its finite local rule predicate. -> Finite nonrecursive tables simulating the next parameter.
- **step-faithful-simulation** [claim](#stmt-fr-faithful) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-faithful): Simulate accepting transactions and read the committed data throughout every interleaving. -> Simulation of upper rules and preservation of nonzero encoded words.
- **step-fragment-bounds** [claim](#stmt-fr-fragment-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-fragment-bound): Partition palettes by control fields and use duplicate-color zeros; bound consecutive blocks without a new cut. -> Bounded palette fragments and bounded cut-free ends.
- **step-token-normalization** [claim](#stmt-fr-token-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fr-token-normalization): Locate the unique transaction within a cut interval, clear checkers, roll back, release, and sort complete palettes. -> Canonical upper encoding obtained with support inside the interval.
- **step-hierarchy-properties** [claim](#stmt-fr-prop-runs) / [proof](#fr:tokens): Combine finite tables, faithful encodings, bounded fragments, and isolated normalization; extract a long homogeneous palette run. -> All hierarchy properties used by the algebra construction.
- **step-alternating-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-ca-alternating) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ca-alternating): Pair permutations with repeated arguments and expand multilinearly in a matrix-space basis. -> Vanishing permutation sum.
- **step-direct-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-ca-direct) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ca-direct): Choose a color-independent long run and partition chunk sequences into free packet-permutation orbits. -> Eventual vanishing of the fixed chunk test.
- **step-cut-transfer** [claim](#stmt-ca-cut-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ca-cut-reduction): Use unique cut recognition and internal token identities to express each long letter sum through finitely many bounded end contexts. -> Lower letter-test vanishing.
- **step-chunk-prefix-automaton** [claim](#stmt-ca-automaton) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ca-automaton): Combine equal-label weights and put identity transitions on proper prefixes and chunk weights on closures. -> A letter test containing the exact chunk sum as a fixed block.
- **step-finite-test-termination** [claim](#stmt-ca-all-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ca-all-tests): Compare palette growth with accumulated dimension growth, stop where direct cancellation applies, and descend through the finite reductions. -> Eventual vanishing for every fixed level-zero test.
- **step-matrix-nil-algebra** [claim](#stmt-thm-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-algebra): Use level-zero relations, surviving encodings of unbounded degree, and a central factor-counting indeterminate followed by coefficient extraction. -> Infinite graded finitely presented algebra with a matrix-nil positive-degree ideal.
- **step-nil-radical-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-nil-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-nil-algebra): Restrict the same positive-degree relations to the free nonunital algebra, use finite geometric inverses, and subtract scalar parts. -> Nonunital nil and radical counterexample and algebraic unitization.
- **step-three-factor-identity** [claim](#stmt-fp-three-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-three-factor): Separate mutually centralizing subgroups and expand a conjugated commuting relation. -> Equal nested commutators centralizing their inputs.
- **step-finite-group-presentation** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-three-factor-2): Induct on monomial length, compare splits, establish commutations and involutions, extend additively, and kill the full algebra ideal. -> The displayed ordinary finite presentation of the Steinberg group.
- **step-perfectness-infinitude** [claim](#stmt-fp-perfectness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-perfectness): Express every root as a commutator and distinguish elementary matrices by their parameter entries. -> Perfectness and infinitude when the algebra is infinite.
- **step-matrix-periodicity** [claim](#stmt-fp-matrix-periodicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fp-matrix-periodicity): Lift residue inverses by a finite geometric sum and kill the nil part of a residue-order power by successive squaring. -> Invertibility criterion and periodic matrix groups.
- **step-frame-completion** [claim](#stmt-st-linear-algebra) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-linear-algebra): Complete residue columns and perform elimination with unit pivots while preserving noncommutative multiplication order. -> Frame completion and generation by elementary matrices and unit diagonals.
- **step-frame-connectivity** [claim](#stmt-st-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-frames): Adjoin labeled vectors by cones on quotient independence complexes and pass from finite subcomplexes to finite cycles and loops. -> Rational homology vanishing and simple connectivity.
- **step-small-support-presentation** [claim](#stmt-st-small-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-small-presentation): Lift a vertex stabilizer inductively and transport its cosets through frame edges; check backtracks and triangles. -> Presentation of the full matrix group by small-coordinate relations.
- **step-centrality** [claim](#stmt-prop-centrality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-centrality): Check the assigned action on faithful incoming and outgoing strips using a coordinate outside each relation support. -> A matrix-group action forcing kernel conjugation to be trivial.
- **step-elementary-quotient-stability** [claim](#stmt-st-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-quotient): Kill elementary generators, identify diagonal positions by swaps, and observe that the remaining relation list is rank-independent. -> Normality and stabilization of the quotient by elementary matrices.
- **step-rational-splitting** [claim](#stmt-st-finite-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-finite-splitting): Average the multiplication defect for finite subgroups and glue the uniquely determined splittings. -> Unique rational homomorphic splitting.
- **step-extension-classification** [claim](#stmt-st-extensions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-extensions): Lift roots through unique unitriangular splittings and construct the converse extension by a central pushout. -> Natural classification by rational kernel characters.
- **step-exact-coinvariants** [claim](#stmt-st-coinvariants) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-coinvariants): Average over the finite subgroup containing each relation witness and apply exact coinvariants to a free resolution. -> Natural quotient homology comparison.
- **step-oriented-stabilizer-homology** [claim](#stmt-st-stabilizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-stabilizers): Project stabilizers onto complementary blocks and use the rational sign action of a transposition to annihilate orientation coinvariants. -> Vertex stabilizer homology and vanishing higher-frame stabilizer homology in the stated range.
- **step-second-homology-stability** [claim](#stmt-st-h2) / [proof](#proof-stmt-st-h2): Compare the augmented frame double complex with group chains and eliminate the vanishing stabilizer columns in the required total degrees. -> Isomorphism induced by the standard stabilization on second rational homology.
- **step-rational-character-extension** [claim](#stmt-prop-rational-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rational-stability): Dualize the homology isomorphism through the natural central-extension classification. -> Extension of every rational kernel character under stabilization.
- **step-rational-torsion-detection** [claim](#stmt-kt-rational-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-rational-detection): Rationalize the abelian group and choose a basis functional detecting a surviving element. -> A rational character detecting every nontorsion element.
- **step-field-kernel-torsion** [claim](#stmt-kt-field-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-field-kernel): Push out a rational character, split the finite quotient extension, and use perfectness to force that character to vanish. -> Torsion of the ground-field kernel.
- **step-coordinate-permutation-lifts** [claim](#stmt-kt-permutations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-permutations): Lift transpositions by elementary root products and check exact strip actions, then compose. -> Exact permutation conjugation on all roots.
- **step-block-substitution-laws** [claim](#stmt-kt-block-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-block-laws): Expand commuting rectangular root products and lift numerical elimination into exact row and column operations. -> Block Steinberg relations and exact lifted conjugation.
- **step-triangular-faithfulness** [claim](#stmt-kt-triangular-faithfulness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-triangular-faithfulness): Collect roots by increasing positive inner gap and recover each coefficient from the matrix image. -> Faithful triangular subgroup and normalization by equal-inner-index roots.
- **step-triangular-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-kt-triangular-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-triangular-evaluation): Separate diagonal and strictly upper terms, collect the latter, and use faithful trivial matrix image. -> Product of coordinate evaluations at the common diagonal value.
- **step-wrap-removal** [claim](#stmt-kt-wrap-removal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-wrap-removal): Collect wrap corrections using only truncated-factor suffixes and bound endpoint displacement inside a faithful disjoint strip. -> Equality of cyclic and truncated evaluations in the Steinberg group.
- **step-stable-torsion** [claim](#stmt-kt-stable-torsion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-stable-torsion): Apply the grading, choose a power-of-two cyclic size, triangularize the cyclic shift, and compare with the truncated evaluation. -> Torsion of the element after standard stabilization.
- **step-descend-kernel-torsion** [claim](#stmt-kt-kernel-torsion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-kernel-torsion): Extend each rational character to a rank where the element is torsion, then apply rational detection in the original abelian kernel. -> Torsion in every kernel of the stated fixed-rank range.
- **step-periodic-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-kernel-torsion-2): Combine the same algebra’s finite presentation and infinitude with periodic elementary image and torsion kernel. -> Infinite ordinarily finitely presented periodic group.
- **step-kazhdan-consequence** [claim](#stmt-cor-kazhdan) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kt-kernel-torsion-3): Apply the cited property theorem for finitely generated unital rings, then exclude an invariant vector in the infinite group’s regular representation. -> Kazhdan property and nonamenability of the constructed group.

          "anchor": "stmt-fr-prop-runs",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:hierarchy\" label=\"prop:hierarchy\"></span> There are absolute positive integers $c,k$, with $c=10000$ and $k=10$ permitted, with the following property. Fix an integer $D\\geq2$. There is an integer $h_0$ and, for every $$h\\in\\{h_0,h_0D,h_0D^2,\\ldots\\},$$ a finite alphabet $\\Sigma_h$, of cardinality at most $2^{ch}$, and a finite table of elementary replacements and zero declarations of length at most $k$. Write $A_h(K)$ for the resulting unital associative algebra over a field $K$, and write $[w]$ for the image of a word. These tables have the following three properties.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"fr:prop-encoding\" label=\"fr:prop-encoding\"></span> There is a fixed-length substitution $E_h:\\Sigma_{Dh}\\longrightarrow\\Sigma_h^{L_h}$, where $L_h=2q_h+1$. Every elementary relation or zero declaration at parameter $Dh$ is a consequence of the relations at parameter $h$ after substitution. Thus $E_h$ induces a unital algebra homomorphism $A_{Dh}(K)\\longrightarrow A_h(K)$. If a word $w$ is nonzero at parameter $Dh$, then $E_h(w)$ is nonzero at parameter $h$. There is a nonzero one-letter word at every parameter.*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"fr:prop-tokens\" label=\"fr:prop-tokens\"></span> A subset of $\\Sigma_h$ specifies cuts immediately before its letters. There are a bound $B_h$ and a finite list $\\mathcal T_h$ of tokens such that every sufficiently long nonzero word has a unique decomposition $$p\\,t_1\\cdots t_r\\,s,\n     \\qquad t_i\\in\\mathcal T_h,\\qquad |p|,|s|\\leq B_h.$$ Here $p$ is the part before its first cut, $s$ is the part from its last cut, and the $t_i$ are exactly the intervals between consecutive cuts. Cuts are counted only when their following letter occurs in the word. Each token starts with a cut letter and has no other cut letter. For each $t\\in\\mathcal T_h$ there is a nonempty word $u_t$ over $\\Sigma_{Dh}$ such that $$1\\leq |u_t|\\leq k,\n     \\qquad |t|=L_h|u_t|,\n     \\qquad [t]=[E_h(u_t)].$$ The equalities can be effected by replacements supported inside the token. No assertion is made that arbitrary concatenations of tokens are nonzero.*\n\n3.  *Some letters are designated palette letters and have a color in a set of $q_h$ colors. An eligible run consists of consecutive palette letters with a common tuple of non-color fields and with neither of the two control-track head flags set. Every adjacent transposition within such a run is an elementary replacement of the whole letters. Every eligible run in a nonzero word has pairwise distinct colors. Every sufficiently long nonzero word contains an eligible run of length at least $q_h/100$. Eligibility and the endpoints of maximal eligible runs depend only on the non-color fields.*\n\n*The integer $D$ is chosen before the program fixed point used to define the tables; $h_0$ is chosen afterward. The constants $c,k$ do not depend on $D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-247.2"
        }
      ],

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-algebra",
          "exact_quote": "There is an infinite, finitely presented, graded unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra $$R=\\bigoplus_{n\\geq0}R_n=\\mathbb F_2\\oplus I,\n  \\qquad R_0=\\mathbb F_2,\\qquad I=\\bigoplus_{n\\geq1}R_n,$$ such that $M_\\ell(I)$ is nil for every integer $\\ell\\geq1$: each matrix with entries in $I$ is nilpotent, with its own nilpotence index.",
          "source_id": "PM-247.2"
        }
      ],

        "Replacement-class basis",

      "output": "Infinite graded finitely presented algebra with a matrix-nil positive-degree ideal.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-algebra"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cor-nil-algebra",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-matrix-nil-algebra"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-nil-algebra",
          "exact_quote": "For the algebra $R=\\mathbb F_2\\oplus I$ of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:algebra\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:algebra\">18</a>, the following hold.*\n\n1.  *The positive-degree ideal $I$, regarded as a nonunital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra, is finitely presented, infinite-dimensional, and nil, but $I^m\\ne0$ for every integer $m\\geq1$. In particular, it is a finitely presented Jacobson radical algebra that is not nilpotent.*\n\n2.  *The algebra $R$ is the unitization of $I$ and is an infinite-dimensional finitely presented algebraic unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra. More precisely, if $r=a1+i$ with $a\\in\\mathbb F_2$ and $i\\in I$, there is an integer $N=N(i)\\geq1$ such that $$(r-a1)^N=i^N=0.$$ Thus $r$ satisfies the nonzero monic polynomial $(t-a)^N$.",
          "source_id": "PM-247.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-nil-radical-consequences",
      "inputs": [
        "Matrix-nil algebra",
        "Positive-degree defining relations"

      "method": "Restrict the same positive-degree relations to the free nonunital algebra, use finite geometric inverses, and subtract scalar parts.",
      "output": "Nonunital nil and radical counterexample and algebraic unitization.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-nil-algebra"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-fp-three-factor",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fp-three-factor",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X,Y,Z$ belong to a group, and put $A=[X,Y]$ and $B=[Y,Z]$. Assume that $A$ commutes with $X$ and $Y$, that $B$ commutes with $Y$ and $Z$, and that $A$ commutes with $B$ and $X$ commutes with $Z$. Then $$[A,Z]=[X,B].$$ Moreover, this common element centralizes each of $A,Z,X,B$.",
          "source_id": "PM-247.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-three-factor-identity",
      "inputs": [
        "Stated commuting assumptions"
      ],
      "method": "Separate mutually centralizing subgroups and expand a conjugated commuting relation.",
      "output": "Equal nested commutators centralizing their inputs.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-fp-three-factor"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-presentation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-three-factor-identity"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-finite-presentation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $$R=T/\\mathfrak a,\n \\qquad\n T=\\mathbb F_2\\langle\\mathcal A\\rangle,\n \\qquad\n \\mathfrak a=(f_1,\\ldots,f_s)$$ be a finite presentation as a unital associative $\\mathbb F_2$-algebra, where $\\mathfrak a$ denotes the two-sided ideal generated by the listed polynomials. If $n\\geq5$, then $\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ has the following ordinary finite group presentation.*\n\n\n\n\n*The generators are $$g_{ij}(w),\\qquad i\\neq j,\\quad w\\in\\mathcal A^*,\\quad |w|\\leq2.$$ To specify the relators, define a formal group word $\\xi_{ij}(w)$ in these generators for every $w\\in\\mathcal A^*$. For $|w|\\leq2$, put $\\xi_{ij}(w)=g_{ij}(w)$. For $|w|>2$, write $w=av$, with $a\\in\\mathcal A$ its first letter, choose the least $h\\in\\{1,\\ldots,n\\}\\setminus\\{i,j\\}$, and put $$\\xi_{ij}(w)=[\\xi_{ih}(a),\\xi_{hj}(v)].$$ This recursion terminates and gives a finite group word for each $w$. The defining relations are $$\\begin{aligned}\n g_{ij}(w)^2&=1,\n && |w|\\leq2, \\label{fp:finite-square}\\\\\n [g_{ij}(u),g_{kl}(v)]&=1,\n && |u|,|v|\\leq2,\\quad j\\neq k,\\ i\\neq l,\n \\label{fp:finite-commutation}\\\\\n [g_{ij}(u),g_{jk}(v)]&=g_{ik}(uv),\n && |u|+|v|\\leq2,\\quad i,j,k\\text{ pairwise distinct},\n \\label{fp:finite-product}\\\\\n \\prod_{w\\in S_{f_t}}\\xi_{ij}(w)&=1,\n && 1\\leq t\\leq s,\\quad i\\neq j.\n \\label{fp:finite-algebra-relators}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The product in Equation <a href=\"#fp:finite-algebra-relators\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"fp:finite-algebra-relators\">[fp:finite-algebra-relators]</a> is taken in the fixed order above, and an empty product is $1$. The isomorphism to $\\mathop{\\mathrm{St}}_n(R)$ sends $g_{ij}(w)$ to $x_{ij}(\\overline w)$, where $\\overline w$ is the image of $w$ in $R$.",
          "source_id": "PM-247.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-finite-group-presentation",

        "Three-factor identity"

      "output": "The displayed ordinary finite presentation of the Steinberg group.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-fp-three-factor-2"

Suppose that $M_d(I)$ is a nil algebra for every positive integer $d$; that is, each individual matrix in $M_d(I)$ is nilpotent. Then a square matrix over $R$ is invertible if and only if its reduction modulo $I$ is invertible. Moreover, every element of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_n(R)$ has finite order, and therefore $\\mathop{\\mathrm{E}}_n(R)$ is periodic.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-segmentation-block**: The distinguished block of the letter-matrix product equals the ordered sum of weights of every segmentation of the input word into chunk labels.
- **inv-duplicate-identifiers**: Combining identifiers with the same label by adding their weights preserves the chunk sum, including repeated occurrences of that label.
- **inv-prefix-state-dimension**: The empty prefix and the nonempty proper prefixes of labels supply the automaton states, with the letter-test dimension bounded as stated in the chunk automaton lemma.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-discard-duplicate-identifiers**: Deduplicate identical labels by retaining only their latest matrix weight. -> Different identifiers contribute separately; replacing addition by overwriting changes the segmentation sum.
- **anti-exclusive-prefix-or-label**: Permit a prefix extension or a chunk closure exclusively when the extended prefix is itself a label. -> A label that is also a proper prefix supports both choices, and discarding either choice loses valid segmentations.
- **anti-commute-matrix-weights**: Reverse or reorder chunk-weight products while accumulating a word. -> Matrix multiplication need not commute, so the result can change even when the concatenated labels remain unchanged.
- **anti-certify-global-nilness-by-sampling**: Treat agreement on finite automaton examples as a certificate of eventual vanishing for every test or of the infinite algebra construction. -> The executable comparison checks the finite prefix-state construction only; the hierarchy and its eventual-vanishing argument remain outside that comparison.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The chunk automaton lemma gives a finite prefix-state construction. The executable specialization compares its distinguished block against exhaustive enumeration of chunk-identifier segmentations over the prime field; it does not certify the infinite group or the eventual-vanishing theorem.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON object containing alphabet, d, labels, word, and an optional name.
# PRECONDITIONS: A nonempty finite alphabet of distinct characters; positive d; nonempty labels over the alphabet; square binary weight matrices.
# OUTPUTS: The binary matrix in the empty-prefix block of the ordered letter-matrix product.
# TIME: Finite prefix construction followed by exact dense matrix products along the input word.
# SPACE: Finite prefix transition matrices and the current product.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer matrix arithmetic reduced modulo two.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce no successful result.
import json
import sys
from sympy import Matrix, eye, zeros

def main():
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    alphabet = data['alphabet']
    d = data['d']
    word = data['word']
    labels = data['labels']
    assert isinstance(alphabet, str) and alphabet and len(set(alphabet)) == len(alphabet)
    assert type(d) is int and d > 0
    assert isinstance(word, str) and all(a in alphabet for a in word)
    assert isinstance(labels, list)
    weights = {}
    for item in labels:
        v = item['word']
        raw = item['weight']
        assert isinstance(v, str) and v and all(a in alphabet for a in v)
        assert isinstance(raw, list) and len(raw) == d
        assert all(isinstance(row, list) and len(row) == d for row in raw)
        assert all(type(x) is int and x in (0, 1) for row in raw for x in row)
        w = Matrix(raw)
        weights[v] = (weights.get(v, zeros(d, d)) + w).applyfunc(lambda x: x % 2)
    prefixes = {''}
    for v in weights:
        prefixes.update(v[:i] for i in range(1, len(v)))
    states = [''] + sorted(prefixes - {''})
    state_index = {p: i for i, p in enumerate(states)}
    size = len(states) * d
    transitions = {}
    for a in alphabet:
        block_matrix = zeros(size, size)
        for p in states:
            pa = p + a
            source = state_index[p] * d
            if pa in state_index:
                target = state_index[pa] * d
                for i in range(d):
                    block_matrix[source + i, target + i] += 1
            if pa in weights:
                for i in range(d):
                    for j in range(d):
                        block_matrix[source + i, j] += weights[pa][i, j]
        transitions[a] = block_matrix.applyfunc(lambda x: x % 2)
    acc = eye(size)
    for a in word:
        acc = (acc * transitions[a]).applyfunc(lambda x: x % 2)
    answer = [[int(acc[i, j]) for j in range(d)] for i in range(d)]
    print(json.dumps(answer, separators=(',', ':')))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

        "input": "{\"name\":\"overlapping-segmentations\",\"alphabet\":\"a\",\"d\":1,\"labels\":[{\"word\":\"a\",\"weight\":[[1]]},{\"word\":\"aa\",\"weight\":[[1]]}],\"word\":\"aaa\"}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "[[0]]",
        "input": "{\"name\":\"incomplete-prefix\",\"alphabet\":\"ab\",\"d\":1,\"labels\":[{\"word\":\"ab\",\"weight\":[[1]]}],\"word\":\"a\"}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "[[1]]",
        "input": "{\"name\":\"empty-list-empty-word\",\"alphabet\":\"a\",\"d\":1,\"labels\":[],\"word\":\"\"}"
      },
      {
        "expected": "[[0,0],[0,1]]",
        "input": "{\"name\":\"reverse-word-order\",\"alphabet\":\"ab\",\"d\":2,\"labels\":[{\"word\":\"a\",\"weight\":[[0,1],[0,0]]},{\"word\":\"b\",\"weight\":[[0,0],[1,0]]}],\"word\":\"ba\"}"
      }
    ]
  },
  "pm_id": "PM-247.2",
  "schema": "pm.triptych.v2"
}

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The addressable computation tableau supplies a concrete procedure for generating an indexed verification clause without enumerating the preceding clauses or the complete computation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The palette and transaction dynamics establish properties of algebraic word replacements; they do not specify a game model, simulation state interface, or physical evolution contract. |
| tools | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The finite Steinberg presentation gives a direct symbolic compilation procedure from a finite associative algebra presentation to ordinary group generators and relator words. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The prefix-state construction converts a finite chunk test into letter matrices whose distinguished block reproduces the chunk sum, including ambiguous segmentations and repeated labels. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A finite collection of proper label prefixes provides a concrete state representation that shares prefixes while preserving weighted chunk closures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The matrix dimension estimate bounds an algebraic automaton representation; it supplies no circuit architecture, memory-layout specification, or accelerator performance result. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The periodic group construction establishes existence and algebraic properties without supplying a cryptographic primitive, security assumption, or hardness reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The letter weights are prescribed numerical matrices used in exact cancellation identities; the paper gives no learning objective, training procedure, or statistical prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The comparison of palette growth and test-dimension growth proves termination of a mathematical reduction, without an optimisation objective or a method for finding an optimal hierarchy. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Transaction ownership and interleaving safety concern local reversible word replacements inside complete blocks. They do not provide a distributed protocol with communication, failures, or recovery semantics. |
| other (Formal verification of reversible local computations) | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The fixed-payload verifier has explicit reversible tours, gate conditions, and zero-declared interfaces, providing a concrete finite transition system for checking soundness and rollback at a fixed admissible parameter. |
